I couldn't really say. I don't pay much attention to what other people eat, or... how long it takes. So, I don't know. I haven't really timed it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to get rid of the extra calories after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I just... I just go back to sleep after.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating very quickly during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I'm not really rushing or anything. I just... eat it at a normal pace, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these late-night eating episodes are causing problems in your life?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say problems, exactly. I mean, I still get things done. I run my errands, make it to my appointments... help out around the house just fine. \n\nIt's just... I guess the mornings after I do it, I feel pretty sluggish. And... well, maybe a little less proud of how I'm handling myself. But I get by.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what you're eating or how much you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: ...I guess you could say that. It's... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those leftovers late at night because you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't really think so. \n\nI'm not... I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person. I don't really get too worked up about things. It's just... I'm awake, and I end up in the kitchen. I don't think it's because I'm upset.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating those leftovers late at night without thinking about the consequences or how it might affect you the next day?\n\nPatient: ...No. I wouldn't say that. \n\nI know how I'm going to feel the next day. Like I mentioned, I know I should stop while I'm doing it. I just... don't.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I haven't really timed it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to get rid of the extra calories after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I just... I just go back to sleep after.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating very quickly during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I'm not really rushing or anything. I just... eat it at a normal pace, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these late-night eating episodes are causing problems in your life?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say problems, exactly. I mean, I still get things done. I run my errands, make it to my appointments... help out around the house just fine. \n\nIt's just... I guess the mornings after I do it, I feel pretty sluggish. And... well, maybe a little less proud of how I'm handling myself. But I get by.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what you're eating or how much you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: ...I guess you could say that. It's... well, that's sort of why I decided to come in. \n\nOnce I start picking at the leftovers in the kitchen, it's like... it feels like the sensible part of me is standing there saying, \"That's enough, Alejandro.\" But... my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. It's... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those leftovers late at night because you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't really think so. \n\nI'm not... I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person. I don't really get too worked up about things. It's just... I'm awake, and I end up in the kitchen. I don't think it's because I'm upset.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating those leftovers late at night without thinking about the consequences or how it might affect you the next day?\n\nPatient: ...No. I wouldn't say that. \n\nI know how I'm going to feel the next day. Like I mentioned, I know I should stop while I'm doing it. I just... don't.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat more food during these late-night episodes than you would during a normal meal?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah, I suppose so. \n\nSome nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be putting away the food... the tamales or the mole... I’ll end up eating several servings. And then maybe some pan dulce out in the garage. It's... definitely more than I’d ever put on a plate if the family was sitting there watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel emotionally upset, such as sad or angry, when you start eating those leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't think so.\n\nLike I said, I'm not really an emotional guy. I'm just... putting the food away. I don't feel sad or mad or anything. It's just quiet in the house.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating those leftovers late at night because you feel an intense urge to do so?\n\nPatient: ...No. I wouldn't call it that. \n\nIt's not some... intense urge. It's... well, that's sort of why I decided to come in. \n\nOnce I start picking at the leftovers in the kitchen, it's like... it feels like the sensible part of me is standing there saying, \"That's enough, Alejandro.\" But... my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. It's... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those leftovers late at night because you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't really think so. \n\nI'm not... I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person. I don't really get too worked up about things. It's just... I'm awake, and I end up in the kitchen. I don't think it's because I'm upset.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating those leftovers late at night without thinking about the consequences or how it might affect you the next day?\n\nPatient: ...No. I wouldn't say that. \n\nI know how I'm going to feel the next day. Like I mentioned, I know I should stop while I'm doing it. I just... don't.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat more food during these late-night episodes than you would during a normal meal?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah, I suppose so. \n\nSome nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be putting away the food... the tamales or the mole... It's... definitely more than I’d ever put on a plate if the family was sitting there watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel emotionally upset, such as sad or angry, when you start eating those leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't think so.\n\nLike I said, I'm not really an emotional guy. I'm just... putting the food away. I don't feel sad or mad or anything. It's just quiet in the house.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating those leftovers late at night because you feel an intense urge to do so?\n\nPatient: ...No. I wouldn't call it that. \n\nIt's not some... intense urge. The food is just sitting right there while I'm cleaning up, and... I just start eating it. I don't really feel a big need to do it beforehand or anything. It just happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry during the day?\n\nPatient: ...I suppose I do. Just normal hunger, I guess. I eat my regular meals. Sometimes after supper, when I'm putting away the food... like the tamales or mole... I end up eating a lot. I'll take it out to the garage, maybe have some pan dulce too. It's... it's definitely more than I would ever put on a plate if my family was around.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've ever eaten an amount of food that's larger than what most people would eat in a short time, like within a couple of hours?\n\nPatient: I... I couldn't really say. I don't pay much attention to what other people eat, or... how long it takes. So, I don't know. I haven't really timed it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to get rid of the extra calories after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I just... I just go back to sleep after.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating very quickly during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I'm not really rushing or anything. I just... eat it at a normal pace, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these late-night eating episodes are causing problems in your life?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say problems, exactly. I mean, I still get things done. I run my errands, make it to my appointments... help out around the house just fine. \n\nIt's just... I guess the mornings after I do it, I feel pretty sluggish. And... well, maybe a little less proud of how I'm handling myself. But I get by.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over what you're eating or how much you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: ...I guess you could say that. It's... well, that's sort of why I decided to come in. \n\nOnce I start picking at the leftovers in the kitchen, it's like... it feels like the sensible part of me is standing there saying, \"That's enough, Alejandro.\" But... my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. It's... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those leftovers late at night because you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't really think so. \n\nI'm not... I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person. I don't really get too worked up about things. It's just... I'm awake, and I end up in the kitchen. I don't think it's because I'm upset.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating those leftovers late at night without thinking about the consequences or how it might affect you the next day?\n\nPatient: ...No. I wouldn't say that. \n\nI know how I'm going to feel the next day. Like I mentioned, I know I should stop while I'm doing it. I just... don't.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat more food during these late-night episodes than you would during a normal meal?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah, I suppose so. \n\nSome nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be putting away the food... the tamales or the mole... I’ll end up eating several servings. And then maybe some pan dulce out in the garage. It's... definitely more than I’d ever put on a plate if the family was sitting there watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel emotionally upset, such as sad or angry, when you start eating those leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't think so.\n\nLike I said, I'm not really an emotional guy. I'm just... putting the food away. I don't feel sad or mad or anything. It's just quiet in the house.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating those leftovers late at night because you feel an intense urge to do so?\n\nPatient: ...No. I wouldn't call it that. \n\nIt's not some... intense urge. The food is just sitting right there while I'm cleaning up, and... I just start eating it. I don't really feel a big need to do it beforehand or anything. It just happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry during the day?\n\nPatient: ...I suppose I do. Just normal hunger, I guess. I eat my regular meals. Nothing really unusual about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel full during the day and not very hungry for your regular meals?\n\nPatient: ...No, not really. Like I said, I eat my meals fine. I don't really skip them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be eating less during the day than you normally would, which then leads to eating more at night?\n\nPatient: ...No, I don't think so. I just... don't.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat more food during these late-night episodes than you would during a normal meal?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah, I suppose so. \n\nSome nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be putting away the food... the tamales or the mole... I’ll end up eating several servings. And then maybe some pan dulce out in the garage. It's... definitely more than I’d ever put on a plate if the family was sitting there watching me.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel emotionally upset, such as sad or angry, when you start eating those leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't think so.\n\nLike I said, I'm not really an emotional guy. I'm just... putting the food away. I don't feel sad or mad or anything. It's just quiet in the house.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating those leftovers late at night because you feel an intense urge to do so?\n\nPatient: ...No. I wouldn't call it that. \n\nIt's not some... intense urge. The food is just sitting right there while I'm cleaning up, and... I just start eating it. I don't really feel a big need to do it beforehand or anything. It just happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry during the day?\n\nPatient: ...I suppose I do. Just normal hunger, I guess. I eat my regular meals. Nothing really unusual about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel full during the day and not very hungry for your regular meals?\n\nPatient: ...No, not really. Like I said, I eat my meals fine. I'm hungry enough for them. I don't feel full all day or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you skip meals during the day?\n\nPatient: ...No. I eat breakfast, lunch, and dinner. I don't really skip them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be eating less during the day than you normally would, which then leads to eating more at night?\n\nPatient: ...No, I don't think so. I just eat my regular amount during the day. I don't think I'm holding back or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah, I guess so. It's... well, once I start picking at the leftovers late at night, it's hard to stop. It feels like the sensible part of me is saying, \"That's enough, Alejandro,\" but... my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. I don't know why.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be restricting your food intake during the day, which then leads to overeating at night?\n\nPatient: ...I thought we just went over that. No. I'm not restricting anything during the day. I just eat my normal meals."
